### GNoME (Graph Networks for Materials Exploration)

founded 2023 · GB · origin: corporate · openness 1→1 · public · confidence: medium · tool: WebFetch (search budget exhausted)

*Computed:* age 3 yrs · 1 closing / 0 opening moves

**What openness did.** GNoME's predictions spread through the Materials Project, but the non-commercial terms and missing weights limit reuse. Unlike AF2, it did not become a tool others run. The open MLIP ecosystem (MACE, CHGNet, Orb, UMA) instead grew from public data.

**Takeaway.** GNoME is the materials-science counterpart of AlphaFold 3's restriction: corporate model, public training data (Materials Project), non-commercial outputs, no weights. It also imported a non-commercial carve-out into a public database.

**Timeline.** None: Materials Project content offered under CC BY 4.0 → Materials Project with a non-commercial, access-controlled GNoME subset (closing)

**Risk events.** Non-commercial data inside a public DOE database; No weights, so predictions cannot be reproduced or extended independently

**Robustness.** Data are mirrored in the Materials Project, but under non-commercial terms; the model itself stays with Google.

**Physical-science relevance.** Inorganic materials discovery: external researchers 'independently synthesized 736' GNoME-predicted materials, and Berkeley Lab's A-Lab synthesised more than 41 new materials using GNoME stability insights.

### M3GNet and MatGL (Materials Graph Library)

founded 2022 · US · origin: academic · openness 4→4 · nonprofit · confidence: medium · tool: WebFetch (search budget exhausted)

*Computed:* age 4 yrs · 0 closing / 0 opening moves

**What openness did.** Permissive licensing let a corporate lab (Microsoft MatterSim) build directly on the architecture, and let an industrial partner (Intel Labs) co-develop the PyTorch port. The cost of openness here is maintenance: two framework migrations in four years.

**Takeaway.** Open academic architectures become the substrate for corporate models. Durability depends on continued maintenance more than on the licence.

**Risk events.** Framework churn: TensorFlow repo archived (2025), DGL backend dropped (2026)

**Robustness.** BSD code, public training data, and a corporate derivative (MatterSim) that shows the architecture's reuse.

**Physical-science relevance.** Universal potentials and property prediction across the periodic table; early demonstration that one MLIP can cover 89 elements.

### MACE foundation models (MACE-MP-0 and successors)

founded 2023 · GB · origin: academic · openness 4→2 · nonprofit · confidence: medium · tool: WebFetch (search budget exhausted)

*Computed:* age 3 yrs · 1 closing / 0 opening moves

**What openness did.** The MIT MACE-MP-0 helped establish universal MLIPs as open tools: 1.4k stars and an 88-author validation paper. Moving later models to a non-commercial licence follows the open-core pattern seen in startups, though here the motive is presumably institutional licensing income (not confirmed).

**Takeaway.** Academic does not always mean permissive. The same group publishes MIT and non-commercial models. Licence choice for models trained on CC BY data is unconstrained, so it reflects the developer's commercialisation strategy.

**Timeline.** None: MIT-licensed foundation models (MP-0, MPA-0) → Academic Software License (non-commercial) for later foundation models (closing)

**Risk events.** Non-commercial licence on newest models limits industrial uptake

**Robustness.** MIT code and MIT MP-0/MPA-0 weights remain; training data public, so anyone can retrain.

**Physical-science relevance.** Universal atomistic simulation across the periodic table; a widely used academic MLIP family.

### Matbench and Matbench Discovery (materials ML benchmarks)

founded 2020 · US · origin: government · openness 4→4 · government · confidence: medium · tool: WebFetch (search budget exhausted)

**What openness did.** Open leaderboards produce comparable numbers and so drive competition among universal MLIPs. Their legitimacy depends on staying neutral and open, which a public lab is well placed to provide.

### MatterGen (Microsoft Research)

founded 2025 · US · origin: corporate · openness 4→4 · public · confidence: high · tool: WebFetch (search budget exhausted)

*Computed:* age 1 yrs · 0 closing / 0 opening moves

**What openness did.** Microsoft released MatterGen fully open, unlike DeepMind's GNoME. Its 1.9k stars put it among the most-starred generative materials models, but code and weights came about 13 months after the preprint.

**Takeaway.** A corporate lab can release a physical-science model fully open (MIT code and weights, open training data) when the business (Azure) does not depend on the model. Compare the same lab's MatterSim, whose best versions are reserved for Azure.

**Robustness.** Fully forkable: MIT code and weights plus CC BY-derived training data.

**Physical-science relevance.** Inverse design of inorganic materials with property conditioning, with a synthesised validation compound.

### MatterSim (Microsoft Research)

founded 2024 · US · origin: corporate · openness 2→2 · public · confidence: medium · tool: WebFetch (search budget exhausted)

*Computed:* age 2 yrs · 0 closing / 0 opening moves

**What openness did.** Open small checkpoints serve as a funnel to Azure Quantum Elements. Its 612 stars are well below the fully open MatterGen from the same lab (1.9k).

**Takeaway.** Within one corporate lab, the model that sits next to a cloud product (MatterSim) is open core, while the one that does not (MatterGen) is fully open.

**Risk events.** Best versions tied to a commercial cloud product

**Robustness.** Small open checkpoints are forkable; no training data to retrain them.

**Physical-science relevance.** Atomistic simulation across elements, temperatures and pressures; model card warns of low accuracy for organic polymers.

### Meta FAIR Chemistry: Open Catalyst, OMat24, OMol25 and UMA

founded 2020 · US · origin: corporate · openness 3→2 · public · confidence: medium · tool: WebFetch (search budget exhausted)

*Computed:* age 6 yrs · 2 closing / 0 opening moves

**What openness did.** Meta's CC BY datasets became the shared training substrate of the field: OMat24 and OMol25 underpin Orb, MACE, SevenNet and UMA itself. Meta kept partial control by putting custom, gated licences on its own weights.

**Takeaway.** Meta is the most generous corporate data publisher in materials and chemistry (hundreds of millions of DFT calculations, CC BY 4.0). It uses Llama-style custom licences for the models. Data openness, not weight openness, has had the larger spillover.

**Timeline.** 2024: MIT-licensed code and openly provided baselines (OC20 era) → Custom, gated checkpoint licence (OMat24 License) (closing); 2025: OMat24 License → FAIR Chemistry License v1 (commercial use allowed, acceptable-use policy, gated; OMol25 repo blocked in China, Russia, Belarus) (closing)

**Risk events.** Custom licence and gating on weights; geographic restrictions; Open Catalyst Project described in the past tense ('was a collaborative research effort')

**Robustness.** CC BY 4.0 datasets let others train independent models (Orb, MACE, SevenNet were trained on OMat24/OMol25), so the ecosystem does not depend on Meta's checkpoints.

**Physical-science relevance.** Catalysis, inorganic materials, molecular chemistry and MOFs for carbon capture; OMol25 is among the largest open quantum-chemistry datasets, hosted with a DOE lab (Argonne).

### NVIDIA BioNeMo Framework and ALCHEMI Toolkit-Ops

founded 2024 · US · origin: corporate · openness 2→2 · public · confidence: low · tool: WebFetch (search budget exhausted)

*Computed:* age 2 yrs · 0 closing / 0 opening moves

**What openness did.** NVIDIA open-sources the software layer for physical-science AI to sell compute. The frameworks are permissive, but adoption is modest (under 1k stars) next to the academic models they accelerate.

**Takeaway.** The hardware vendor's open science is complementary-goods strategy: Apache-2.0 frameworks and accelerated versions of other people's open models, with value captured in GPUs and enterprise services.

**Risk events.** Open code optimised for one vendor's GPUs; the commercial layer (NIM) is not documented on fetched pages

**Robustness.** Apache-2.0 code can be forked, but performance depends on NVIDIA hardware and libraries.

**Physical-science relevance.** Accelerates training and inference of open biology models (ESM-2, Geneformer, AMPLIFY, Evo 2 recipes) and atomistic simulation kernels for MLIPs.

### OpenFold and OpenFold3

founded 2021 · US · origin: academic · openness 3→4 · nonprofit · confidence: medium · tool: WebFetch (search budget exhausted)

*Computed:* age 5 yrs · 0 closing / 2 opening moves

**What openness did.** OpenFold turned AF2 from a usable tool into a reproducible one, publishing the training set (OpenProteinSet) that DeepMind did not. It then repeated the move for AF3. Its role is infrastructure for academic and pharma users rather than a product.

**Takeaway.** Academic and consortium reproductions are how restricted corporate models become open science. OpenFold shows that the training data (MSAs) and the training code, not only the weights, are the scarce public goods.

**Timeline.** 2022: Code-only reimplementation → Code plus trained weights plus training database (opening); 2025: AlphaFold 3 restricted (server only, then non-commercial weights) → OpenFold3-preview under Apache-2.0 (opening); fork: OpenFold3

**Risk events.** OpenFold3 is a 'preview'; its weight licence was not stated on the fetched page

**Robustness.** Permissive code and CC BY training data on AWS Open Data allow independent retraining; consortium rather than single-vendor control.

**Physical-science relevance.** Protein and complex structure prediction; the open training set lets labs retrain or fine-tune on their own data.

### Orb models (Orbital Materials)

founded 2024 · GB · origin: corporate · openness 3→3 · private_vc · confidence: medium · tool: WebFetch (search budget exhausted)

*Computed:* age 2 yrs · 0 closing / 0 opening moves

**What openness did.** A startup used Apache-2.0 models trained on public data as visibility and recruiting assets, while selling proprietary discovery services. The permissive weights make Orb the commercially usable option on OMat24/OMol25 data.

**Takeaway.** Startups in materials AI tend to open the model and close the application, the reverse of the protein startups (Chai, Boltz) that open older models and close the newest.

**Robustness.** Apache-2.0 weights; trained on public data, so retraining is possible in principle.

**Physical-science relevance.** Fast universal MLIPs for materials and molecular simulation; used commercially for cooling and carbon-capture materials (company).

### RoseTTAFold and RFdiffusion family (Baker lab)

founded 2021 · US · origin: academic · openness 1→3 · nonprofit · confidence: medium · tool: WebFetch (search budget exhausted)

*Computed:* age 5 yrs · 0 closing / 1 opening moves

**What openness did.** The Baker lab opened up over time, while DeepMind (AF2 to AF3) closed. Weights went from non-commercial (2021) to BSD (2023 onward), and its design tools are among the most forked in the field.

**Takeaway.** An academic lab chose permissive weight licences for its generative design models, the opposite trajectory to the corporate lab. That made RFdiffusion and the MPNN models the open default for protein design.

**Timeline.** 2023: Non-commercial Rosetta-DL weights (RoseTTAFold) → BSD-licensed weights (RFdiffusion; text clarified 2023-10-03) (opening)

**Risk events.** Original RoseTTAFold weights remain non-commercial; Model generations move between repositories (RoseTTAFold, RFdiffusion, RFAA, Foundry)

**Robustness.** Permissive BSD weights for current models; university copyright; several repositories with hundreds of forks.

**Physical-science relevance.** De novo protein and binder design and protein-nucleic acid design (RFD3NA), feeding wet-lab protein engineering.

### SevenNet (Scalable EquiVariance-Enabled Neural Network)

founded 2023 · KR · origin: academic · openness 4→4 · nonprofit · confidence: medium · tool: WebFetch (search budget exhausted)

*Computed:* age 3 yrs · 0 closing / 1 opening moves

**What openness did.** A Korean academic group moved from copyleft to permissive licensing to widen reuse, which shows licence direction in academic MLIPs is mixed (SevenNet opened while MACE partly closed).

**Takeaway.** Academic MLIP licences are converging on permissive terms, but not uniformly. Check the licence of each checkpoint, not only the code.

**Timeline.** 2025: GPL-3.0 (copyleft) → MIT (permissive) (opening)

**Robustness.** Permissive code since 2025; models trained on public data.

**Physical-science relevance.** Large-scale parallel MD with universal potentials (multi-fidelity training across DFT levels).
